# Title-path rule exceptions: to revisit
User decision (2026-09-28): "if we can get items to 100% byte match for the
title screen do it. Record anything that breaks rules and we will return to
them."
For code on the title-screen path (the recomp checker's rows and their
closure), a byte-exact form may be used and linked even when it breaks the
strict acceptance policy in `docs/CAMPAIGN_OPERATIONS.md`. Every such use must
be:
1. **Tagged in the source**, right at the construct, with
   `RULE-EXCEPTION(title-path): <rule broken> — see docs/RULE_EXCEPTIONS.md`,
   so `grep -rn "RULE-EXCEPTION" src` finds them all.
2. **Listed below**: function, file, the rule it breaks, the form used, and
   what a clean fix would need.
User decision (2026-09-29): **byte matching is the goal for now, everywhere, not
only on the title path.** A form that reaches 100% but breaks the strict policy
is merged, tagged and listed the same way (`RULE-EXCEPTION(title-path)` on the
title path, `RULE-EXCEPTION(user-approved)` elsewhere). The tagged entries are
resolved in a clean-up pass at the end. AGENTS.md's hard rules still apply.

These are not strict wins. They stay on this list until a policy-clean form
replaces them. A row marked user-approved may be off the title path: the user
approved it explicitly for the function it names, and its source tag reads
`RULE-EXCEPTION(user-approved)`. Nothing here overrides AGENTS.md's hard rules: no `.inc`
files, no asm except the evidenced registry, and no extracted assets.

Simplifying the masks and temporaries lost the exact match in local checks. Retained under the byte-match-first policy, not a strict policy-clean win. | Recover natural types and expressions that preserve all instruction bytes and relocations without mutation artifacts |

| fightTrainerSortTemoti (100%, unit fight_range_802331F4, linked) | src/game/fight_range_80211A00.c | Casts and spellings whose only effect is codegen; scalar reuse; local pragma | Several call results carry `(int)` casts and some `(u16)x` / `x & 0xffff` spellings were chosen by an objdiff-scored local search; they change register allocation only. The output-clear loop counts in `uVar5` instead of its own local, which makes the later counter a split web and reproduces retail's separate `li r20,0`. The 0x21 sort keeps its state in locals that are dead in that branch: `r3` is the outer index, `uVar14` the count (set in the for-init with `& 0xffff`, so the bound is a named web), `uVar20` the inner index (a reused local, so its web numbers in the split-web range like retail's), and `uVar13`/`uVar15` the two status values. `uVar15` takes the u16 call result without a `(u16)` cast, so the value goes straight into its named web; with the cast, the conversion temporary numbers late and the base, inner index and bound colours rotate (r20/r22/r25). An earlier version reused `uVar11` for both the bound and the first status value, which clobbered the bound (a behaviour bug, fixed in a152bede8). The two move-power sums go through the `static inline sumPower` helper (used twice); the file's unbalanced `dont_inline` on/off/reset pairs leave inlining disabled at this point, so the helper and function sit inside `#pragma push` / `#pragma dont_inline off` / `#pragma pop`, which only restores the unit default (`-inline auto`). | The original sort routine source |
